Financial instruments: Financial liabilities and equity 
3574 
12.2 Fixed-for-fixed 
On the classification of financial instruments that will or may be settled in the issuer’s own equity instruments, 
the Board’s tentative decisions are reflected in the FICE ED, published in November 2023.40 
The proposals include that for a derivative on own equity to meet the fixed-for-fixed condition in IAS 32, the 
amount of consideration to be exchanged for each of an entity’s own equity instruments is required to be 
denominated in the entity’s functional currency and must be fixed or vary only due to an adjustment which varies 
solely because of: 
• 
Preservation adjustments that require the entity to preserve the relative economic interest of future 
shareholders to an equal or lesser extent than those of existing shareholders. 
Or 
• 
Passage-of-time adjustments that are pre-determined and vary only with the passage of time and have the 
effect of fixing on initial recognition the present value of the amount of consideration exchanged for each of 
the entity’s own equity instruments. 
The ED proposes to classify as equity, a contract that can be settled by exchanging a fixed number of one class of 
an entity’s own non-derivative equity instruments with a fixed number of another class of its own non-derivative 
equity instruments. 
The ED also proposes that in classifying a derivative that gives one party a choice of settlement between two or 
more classes of an entity’s own equity instruments the entity would consider whether the fixed-for-fixed 
condition is met for each class of its own equity instruments that may be delivered. 
12.3 Disclosures for financial instruments that contain obligations that 
arise only on liquidation of the entity 
The FICE ED includes proposed amendments to IFRS 7 to require an entity to disclose information on the nature 
and priority of claims against the entity on liquidation arising from financial liabilities and equity instruments. 
The ED proposes that an entity disclose the carrying amount of each class of claims arising from these financial 
instruments and the line item in the statement of financial position where the claim is included. An entity must 
group the claims into classes based on their contractual nature and priority on liquidation, with specific 
requirements for the separate and consolidated financial statements.41 
The objective is not to provide information about the priority of individual financial instruments on liquidation, 
but rather a categorisation of the capital structure to show differences in the quality of capital within the 
structure to help users assess the nature of the claims and how they affect the entity’s liquidity and solvency. 
The ED proposes that, for financial instruments with both financial liability and equity characteristics (except for 
stand-alone derivatives), an entity would be required to disclose in the notes to the financial statements:42 
• 
‘Debt-like features’ in financial instruments that are classified as equity instruments (for example, fixed 
coupons payable on a preference share). 
• 
‘Equity-like features’ in financial instruments that are classified as financial liabilities (for example, 
embedded derivatives on own equity that do not meet the fixed-for-fixed criterion, or ‘write-down bonds’, 
where the principal is reduced if the issuer’s capital falls below a trigger level). 
• 
The terms and conditions that determine the classification of such financial instruments as financial 
liabilities, equity instruments or compound financial instruments. This includes information about terms that 
become, or stop being effective with the passage of time before the end of the instrument’s contractual 
term. 
• 
The ED also proposes to require entities to disclose the potential dilution of ordinary shares arising from 
financial instruments that could be settled by delivering ordinary shares, such as convertible bonds and 
derivatives on own equity.43 The disclosure also applies to share-based payment transactions to which 
IFRS 2, Share-based Payment applies and would be in addition to the normal IAS 33 Earnings per share 
diluted earnings per share disclosure.

International GAAP® 2025 
Chapter 43 
Financial instruments: Financial liabilities and equity 
3575 
12.4 Accounting for financial instruments that contain contingent 
settlement provisions 
For financial instruments with contingent settlement provisions, proposed changes to IAS 32 were included 
in the FICE ED.44 The ED proposes to clarify the following: 
• 
Financial instruments with contingent settlement provisions may be compound financial instruments 
with liability and equity components. 
• 
The probability and estimated timing of occurrence or non-occurrence of uncertain future events (or 
the outcome of uncertain circumstances) have no effect on the initial and subsequent measurement. 
As a result, the liability component of a compound financial instrument with contingent settlement 
provisions, which could require immediate settlement if a contingent event occurs, is measured on 
initial recognition and subsequently at the present value of the settlement amount. 
• 
Payments at the discretion of the issuer (such as dividends) are recognised in equity, even if all the 
proceeds are initially allocated to the liability component of a compound financial instrument such that 
the equity component has an initial carrying amount of zero. 
• 
The term ‘liquidation’ in paragraph 25(b) of IAS 32 refers to the process that begins after an entity has 
permanently ceased its operations. 
And 
• 
The assessment of whether a contractual term that is ‘not genuine’ under paragraph 25(a) of IAS 32 
requires judgement based on the specific facts and circumstances and is not based on the probability 
or likelihood of the contingent event occurring. 
The accounting for types of contingent settlement provisions that are not embedded in a financial 
instrument would not be subject to these potential future changes to IAS 32. Examples of such contingent 
settlement provision could include those which arise in the context of asset acquisitions in the extractive 
industries (see Chapter 39 at section 8.4.1) and contingent consideration in business combinations (see 
Chapter 10 at section 7.1). 
12.5 The effect of laws or regulations 
For the effect of laws or regulations, proposed changes to IAS 32 were included in the FICE ED.45 
The FICE ED proposes that, when considering the classification of a financial instrument (or its component 
parts) as a financial asset, financial liability or equity instrument, an entity should consider only those 
contractual rights and obligations that are enforceable by law and that are in addition to those created by 
relevant laws or regulations. If a right or obligation is created by relevant laws or regulations, and would 
arise regardless of whether it is included in the contractual arrangement, an entity would not consider that 
right or obligation in its assessment. 
12.6 Shareholders’ discretion 
For shareholders’ discretion, proposed changes to IAS 32 were included in the FICE ED.46 The ED proposes 
to clarify that whether an entity has an unconditional right to avoid delivering cash or another financial 
asset (or otherwise to settle a financial instrument in such a way that it would be a financial liability, see 
section 4.2 above) depends on the facts and circumstances in which shareholder discretion arises. 
Judgement is required to assess whether shareholder decisions are treated as entity decisions. 
 
44 
Exposure Draft: Financial Instruments with Characteristics of Equity - Proposed amendments to IAS 32, IFRS 7 and IAS 1, IAS 32 
paragraphs 16, 22, 22B to 22D, AG27A and AG29B, November 2023, available on the IFRS Foundation’s website. 
45 
Exposure Draft: Financial Instruments with Characteristics of Equity - Proposed amendments to IAS 32, IFRS 7 and IAS 1, IAS 32 
paragraphs 15A and AG24A to AG24B, November 2023, available on the IFRS Foundation’s website.

Exposure Draft: Financial Instruments with Characteristics of Equity - Proposed amendments to IAS 32, IFRS 7 and IAS 1, IAS 32 
paragraphs AG28A to AG28C, November 2023, available on the IFRS Foundation’s website. 


International GAAP® 2025 
Chapter 43 
Financial instruments: Financial liabilities and equity 
3576 
The ED proposes examples of the potential factors an entity would be required to consider in making the 
assessment (each of which would not be determinative on their own), including whether: 
• 
A shareholder decision is a routine part of the operating and corporate governance process (e.g., the 
approval of dividends at an annual general meeting) or not (e.g., the approval of a change of control, 
which will often occur at a ‘special’ meeting and require more than a simple majority). 
• 
The shareholder decision relates to an action proposed or a transaction initiated by the entity’s 
management, so that shareholders merely approve or reject management’s proposals, or if it is 
initiated by the shareholders or third parties. 
• 
The shareholder decision affects different share classes differently, so that, for example, discretion by 
preference shareholders would be treated as indicative of a liability and separate voting by class would 
suggest voting as a class and not as part of the entity. 
• 
A shareholder decision-making right may enable a shareholder to require the entity to redeem or pay a 
return on its shares in cash. Such decision making rights indicate that the shareholders would make 
their decisions as investors in the shares and is unlikely to be an entity decision. 
12.7 Reclassification of financial liabilities and equity instruments 
The proposed changes to IAS 32 for the reclassification of financial liabilities and equity instruments were 
included in the FICE ED.47 The ED proposes to add a general requirement that prohibits reclassification of a 
financial instrument after initial recognition, unless paragraph 16E of IAS 32 applies (see section 4.6.5 
above) or the substance of the contractual terms changes because of a change in circumstances external to 
the contractual arrangement. 
If the substance of the contractual terms change because of changes in circumstance external to the 
contractual arrangement, an entity would: 
• 
Measure a financial liability reclassified from equity at the fair value at the date of reclassification. Any 
difference between the carrying amount of the equity instrument and the fair value of the financial 
liability would be recognised in equity. 
• 
Measure an equity instrument reclassified from a financial liability at the carrying value of the financial 
liability at the date of reclassification. No gain or loss would be recognised on reclassification. 
• 
The reclassification would be accounted for prospectively from the date when the change in 
circumstances occurs. An entity must not reverse in profit or loss any previously recognised income, 
expense, gains or losses. 
The ED also proposes examples of changes in circumstances that are external to the contractual 
arrangement requiring reclassification. 
12.8 Obligations to purchase an entity’s own equity instruments 
For an entity’s obligations to repurchase its own equity instruments (e.g., put options on non-controlling 
interests), the FICE ED proposes clarifications to the existing IAS 32 requirements.48 These are as follows: 
• 
The requirements in IAS 32 paragraph 23 for contracts containing an obligation for an entity to 
purchase its own equity instruments for cash or another financial asset, also apply to contracts for an 
entity to redeem its own equity instruments that will be settled in a variable number of another class of 
the entity’s own equity instruments. [IAS 32.23]. 
• 
On initial recognition of an obligation to redeem an entity’s own equity instruments, if the entity does 
not yet have access to the rights and returns associated with ownership of the equity instruments to 
which the obligation relates, those equity instruments would continue to be recognised. The initial 
amount of the financial liability would be recognised as a debit entry in a component of equity other 
than non-controlling interests (NCI) (in the case of obligations involving NCI) or issued share capital (in 
the case of other obligations to purchase own shares). 
• 
An entity is required to use the same approach for the initial and subsequent measurement of the 
financial liability that arises from a contingent settlement provision. In other words, the entity would 
measure the liability at the present value of the redemption amount and ignore the probability and 
estimated timing of the counterparty exercising that redemption right. 
 
47 
Exposure Draft: Financial Instruments with Characteristics of Equity - Proposed amendments to IAS 32, IFRS 7 and IAS 1, IAS 32 
paragraphs 32B to 32D and AG35A, November 2023, available on the IFRS Foundation’s website. 
48 
Exposure Draft: Financial Instruments with Characteristics of Equity - Proposed amendments to IAS 32, IFRS 7 and IAS 1, IAS 32 
paragraphs 23, AG27B to AG27D, November 2023, available on the IFRS Foundation’s website. 


International GAAP® 2025 
Chapter 43 
Financial instruments: Financial liabilities and equity 
3577 
• 
When remeasuring the financial liability that arises from an obligation to purchase its own equity 
instruments, an entity is required to recognise any gain or loss in profit or loss. 
• 
On expiry without delivery of an obligation for an entity to purchase its own equity instruments: 
• 
The carrying amount of the financial liability would be removed from financial liabilities and 
included in the same component of equity as that from which it was reclassified on initial 
recognition of the financial liability. 
And 
• 
Any gains or losses previously recognised in profit or loss from remeasuring the financial liability 
would not be reversed. However, the entity may transfer the cumulative amount of those gains 
and losses from retained earnings to another component of equity. 
• 
Written put options and forward purchase contracts on an entity’s own equity instruments that are 
gross physically settled are presented gross. This is to align with the accounting for other obligations 
that are conditional on events or choices that are beyond the entity’s control, and to better allow users 
to assess the entity’s exposure to liquidity risk. 
12.9 Presentation of equity instruments and financial liabilities 
The FICE ED includes the proposed amendments to IAS 1 for the presentation of equity instruments and 
financial liabilities.49 For the presentation of equity instruments, the ED proposes amendments to meet the 
needs of users of financial statements (particularly investors in ordinary shares), including transparency 
about whether an entity has issued other instruments classified as equity and to make a clear distinction of 
the returns to ordinary shareholders. The ED proposes to make amounts attributable to ordinary 
shareholders clearly visible in the entity’s financial statements. The proposed amendments are for an entity 
to present: 
• 
Line items in the statement of financial position to show issued capital and reserves attributable 
to ordinary shareholders of the parent separately from issued capital and reserves attributable to other 
owners of the parent. 
• 
The components of equity to be reconciled in the statement of changes in equity for each class of 
ordinary share capital separately from each class of other contributed equity. 
• 
In the statement of comprehensive income, to show an allocation of profit or loss and other 
comprehensive income attributable to ordinary shareholders of the parent separately from the 
respective amounts attributable to other owners of the parent. 
• 
The amount of dividends recognised as distributions to ordinary shareholders separately from 
dividends recognised as distributions to other owners during the period. 
For the presentation of financial liabilities, the IASB considered whether it is appropriate for an entity to 
recognise changes in the carrying amount of a financial liability in profit or loss when the financial liability 
contains a contractual obligation to pay the holder based on the entity’s performance or changes in the 
entity’s net assets. The IASB tentatively decided not to add to the existing presentation requirements for 
financial liabilities but to require disclosure of the total gains or losses in each reporting period that arise 
from measuring such financial liabilities. 
12.10 Disclosures 
The proposed amendments for the disclosure of equity instruments and financial liabilities were included in 
the FICE ED.50 The ED proposes: 
• 
To expand the objective of IFRS 7 to enable users of financial statements to understand how an entity 
is financed and what its current and potential ownership structure is, including potential dilution to the 
ownership structure from financial instruments issued at the reporting date. 
• 
To delete the current references in IFRS 7 paragraph 3(a) which exclude derivatives that meet the 
definition of an equity instrument in IAS 32 from the scope of IFRS 7. 
 
49 
Exposure Draft: Financial Instruments with Characteristics of Equity - Proposed amendments to IAS 32, IFRS 7 and IAS 1, IAS 1 
paragraphs 54, 81B, 107 and 108, November 2023, available on the IFRS Foundation’s website.

• 
To relocate the disclosure requirement currently in IAS 1 paragraph 80A to IFRS 7 and expand it to 
cover reclassifications when changes in the substance of the contractual terms arise from changes in 
circumstances external to the contractual arrangement. An entity would be required to disclose the 
amounts reclassified into and out of financial liabilities or equity, and the timing and reason for that 
reclassification. 
• 
To amend IFRS 7 paragraph 20(a)(i) to require the separate disclosure of gains or losses in each 
reporting period that arise from remeasuring financial liabilities containing contractual obligations 
to pay amounts based on an entity’s performance or changes in the entity’s net assets. 
• 
To include disclosure requirements for compound financial instruments in IFRS 7 paragraph 17A. 
The ED also proposes to require entities to disclose information about obligations that arise only on 
liquidation of the entity, as discussed in section 12.3 above. 
12.11 Transition 
The proposed transition requirements for the amendments to IAS 32, IFRS 7 and IAS 1 were included in the 
FICE ED.51 The ED proposes that for an entity already applying IFRS accounting standards: 
• 
If it is impracticable (as defined in IAS 8) for the entity to apply the effective interest method in IFRS 9 
retrospectively, the entity is required to treat the fair value at the beginning of the earliest 
comparative period presented as the amortised cost of the financial liability at that date. 
• 
The entity is not required to separate the liability and equity components if the liability component 
of a compound financial instrument with a contingent settlement provision was no longer 
outstanding at the date of initial application. 
• 
To require the entity to disclose, in the reporting period that includes the date of initial application 
of the amendments, the nature and amount of any changes in classification resulting from initial 
application of the amendments. This includes the classification and carrying amount of the 
financial instrument immediately before applying the amendments and immediately after applying 
the amendments. 
• 
To provide transition relief from the quantitative disclosures in IAS 8 paragraph 28(f). 
• 
Not to provide any transition relief from the requirements in IAS 34 Interim Financial Reporting for 
interim financial statements issued within the annual period in which the entity first applies the 
amendments. 
• 
For first-time adopters, not to provide any additional transition relief.

Appendix B - Defined terms 
Extract from IFRS standards - Definitions 
derivative 
A financial instrument or other contract within the scope of this 
Standard with all three of the following characteristics. 
(a) its value changes in response to the change in a specified interest 
rate, financial instrument price, commodity price, foreign 
exchange rate, index of prices or rates, credit rating or credit 
index, or other variable, provided in the case of a non-financial 
variable that the variable is not specific to a party to the contract 
(sometimes called the ‘underlying’). 
(b) it requires no initial net investment or an initial net investment 
that is smaller than would be required for other types of contracts 
that would be expected to have a similar response to changes in 
market factors. 
(c) it is settled at a future date. [IFRS 9 Appendix A]. 
equity instrument 
An equity instrument is any contract that evidences a residual 
interest in the assets of an entity after deducting all of its liabilities. 
[IAS 32.11]. 
fair value 
The price that would be received to sell an asset or paid to transfer a 
liability in an orderly transaction between market participants at the 
measurement date. [IFRS 13 Appendix A]. 
financial asset 
A financial asset is any asset that is: 
(a) cash; 
(b) an equity instrument of another entity; 
(c) a contractual right: 
(i) to receive cash or another financial asset from another 
entity; or 
(ii) to exchange financial assets or financial liabilities with 
another entity under conditions that are potentially 
favourable to the entity; or 
(d) a contract that will or may be settled in the entity’s own equity 
instruments and is: 
(i) a non-derivative for which the entity is or may be obliged to 
receive a variable number of the entity’s own equity 
instruments; or 
(ii) a derivative that will or may be settled other than by the 
exchange of a fixed amount of cash or another financial 
asset for a fixed number of the entity’s own equity 
instruments. 
For this purpose the entity’s own equity instruments do not 
include puttable financial instruments classified as equity 
instruments in accordance with paragraphs 16A and 16B, 
instruments that impose on the entity an obligation to 
deliver to another party a pro rata share of the net assets of 
the entity only on liquidation and are classified as equity 
instruments in accordance with paragraphs 16C and 16D, or 
instruments that are contracts for the future receipt or 
delivery of the entity’s own equity instruments. [IAS 32.11]. 
financial instrument 
A financial instrument is any contract that gives rise to a financial 
asset of one entity and a financial liability or equity instrument of 
another entity. [IAS 32.11]. 


International GAAP® 2025 
Chapter 43 
Financial instruments: Financial liabilities and equity 
3581 
financial liability 
A financial liability is any liability that is: 
(a) a contractual obligation: 
(i) to deliver cash or another financial asset to another entity; 
or 
(ii) to exchange financial assets or financial liabilities with 
another entity under conditions that are potentially 
unfavourable to the entity; or 
(b) a contract that will or may be settled in the entity’s own equity 
instruments and is: 
(i) a non-derivative for which the entity is or may be obliged to 
deliver a variable number of the entity’s own equity 
instruments; or 
(ii) a derivative that will or may be settled other than by the 
exchange of a fixed amount of cash or another financial 
asset for a fixed number of the entity’s own equity 
instruments. For this purpose, rights, options or warrants to 
acquire a fixed number of the entity’s own equity 
instruments for a fixed amount of any currency are equity 
instruments if the entity offers the rights, options or 
warrants pro rata to all of its existing owners of the same 
class of its own non-derivative equity instruments. Also, for 
these purposes the entity’s own equity instruments do not 
include puttable financial instruments that are classified as 
equity instruments in accordance with paragraphs 16A and 
16B, instruments that impose on the entity an obligation to 
deliver to another party a pro rata share of the net assets of 
the entity only on liquidation and are classified as equity 
instruments in accordance with paragraphs 16C and 16D, or 
instruments that are contracts for the future receipt or 
delivery of the entity’s own equity instruments. 
As an exception, an instrument that meets the definition of 
a financial liability is classified as an equity instrument if it 
has all the features and meets the conditions in paragraphs 
16A and 16B or paragraphs 16C and 16D. [IAS 32.11]. 
puttable instrument 
A puttable instrument is a financial instrument that gives the holder 
the right to put the instrument back to the issuer for cash or another 
financial asset or is automatically put back to the issuer on the 
occurrence of an uncertain future event or the death or retirement 
of the instrument holder. [IAS 32.11].
